PREFATORY    NOTE. 
7  3  3^ 

Certain  points  have  arisen  during  the  printing  of  the  text  of 

the  Missal  that  require,  perhaps,  a  few  words  of  explanation. 

Unfortunately,  by  an  oversight,  the  stop  7  was  allowed  to 
remain  till  it  was  too  late  to  remove  it.  These  stops  are  rather 
difficult  to  represent  in  print.  In  the  original  it  is  much  shorter 
and  has  a  dot  under  it,  the  whole  of  it  being  above  the  line. 
This  explanation  seems  the  more  necessary  as  in'  many  MSS. 
7  stands  for  et. 

Next  there  are  the  preface  signs.  These  are  three  in  number, 
C£),  (£ ,  V,  and  all  alike  represent  the  first  few  words  of  the 
preface.  Of  these  the  first  is  obviously  a  monogram  of  the 
letters  V  D  for  Vere  dignum.  The  second,  Mr.  Wilson  tells  me, 
is  probably  simply  a  variant  form  of  this  symbol.  But  it 
seems  that  the  scribe  has  regarded  it  occasionally  as  standing 
for  JE  and  so  has  written  C£  terne.  This  I  have  printed 
(E  [e]terne.  In  the  same  way,  where  he  has  written  Vterne 
I  have  printed  V  [ejterne.  He  is,  however,  not  always  con- 
sistent. 

The  Index  of  the  Sequences  given  in  the  Ordinate  I  have 
printed  in  this  vohmie,  as  I  thought  that  it  might  prove  useful, 
more  especially  as  I  have  referred  to  the  Sequences  in  the 
notes  of  this  volume.

1  Ff.  74  and  75,  are  inserted  evidently  for  the  sake  of  the  ^xd^yexAgimus  tibi 
deo  patri,  and  possibly  also  to  include  the  form  Sanguis  domini  nostri  which 
is  in  J.  but  did  not  originally  occur  here.  The  prayer  A  gimus  tibi  was 
originally  written  in  a  small  hand  more  or  less  contemporary  with  the  original 
hand,  at  the  bottom  of  fo.  76  and  is  erased.  It  is  written  in  a  rather  later 
hand  in  the  right-hand  margin  of  fo.  76,  and  was  erased  doubtless  at  the  time 
of  the  insertion  of  the  two  folios.  J  agrees  with  inserted  folios  in  matter 
and  order.  At  the  bottom  of  fo.  736  the  first  line  of  the  prayer  Domiiie 
ihesu  christe,  which  goes  on  in  the  original  in  fo.  76,  is  erased  and  rewritten 
on  the  inserted  page.

NOTE. 

The  chief  abbreviations  used  in  the  notes  are  as  follows : — 

C  .  .      Missale  ad  iisuni  sacri  ordinis  Cistercieiisis,  etc,     Paris,  1584. 

C  Brev.  .  .      MS.  Breviary  of  the  Cistercian  Rite,  belonging  to  the  Bodleian 

Library,  Lat.    liturg.  f.  i.     Century  xiii. 
CC  .  .      The  Cisterican  Consuetudinary,  printed  in  Guignard,  Monuments 

primitifs  de  la  regie  cistercienne.      Dijon,  1878. 
C  Ord.     .  .      The  Cisterican  Ordinale  ;     a  MS.   on  paper  belonging  to  F.   C. 

Eeles,  Esq.,  of  the  fifteenth  century  and  based  entirely  on  the 

Consuetudinary. 
G,  G"-      .  .      Are  the  Gregorian  Sacranientary  and  Supplement.      (Ed.  H.  A. 

Wilson,    H.B.S.,    1915.)      Other    letters    attached    signify   the 

MS.  cited  by  Mr.  Wilson. 
A-C  .  ,      The  fifteenth-century  Missal  of  the  Augustinian  Priory  outside 

Bristol,  and  now  in  the  Public  Library,  Bristol. 
Vict.        .  .      Missale  Can.  Reg.  ordinis  s.  Aiigustini  sec.  rititrn  insignis  ecclesie 

S.  Victoris  ad  muros  Parisienses.     Paris,  1520. 
S  .  ,      Stands    for    the    Sarum    Rite    generally — S-^,    S^     denote     the 

authorities    used    by    Dr.    Wickham    Legg    in    his    edition    of 

The  Sarum  Missal. 
Y,  H       .  ,      Stand  respectively  for  the  York  and  Hereford  books. 
W  .  .      The  Westminster  Missal,  edited  by  Dr.  Wickham  Legg. 

Ross.       .  .      The  Rosslyn  Missal,  edited  by  Dr.  Lawlor. 
Mart^ne.  .      De  antiquis  ecclesicB  Ritibus.     Venice,  1788. 

The  abbreviations  of  the  various  other  authorities,  English  and  French, 
are,  I  think,  obvious.  These  authorities  I  know  only  from  the  notes  of 
Dr.  Wickha.m  Legg  to  his  edition  of  the  Westminster  Missal.

p.  26.      De  5.  Gileherto. 

The  only  other  Use  which  contains  a  Mass  for  S.  Gilbert 
is  that  of  York. 

The  three  Prayers,  we  are  told  in  the  Life  of  S,  Gilbert, 
were  composed  by  Pope  Innocent  II  in  consequence  of  a 
vision  that  was  vouchsafed  to  him  while  the  Canonization 
was  impending. 

"  Statimque  specialem  orationem  de  eo  composuit  quam 
et  Secretum  et  Postcommunionem  quas  postea  edidit 
decreuit  in  commemorationem  ipsius  esse  dicendas.  Oratio 
autem  haec  est  Pleriam  in  nobis,  etc.  Secreta,  Accepta  sit 
tibi,  etc.,  Postcommunio  Quod  ad  te  domine,  etc."  {Vita, 
Dugdale  (1846)  VI,  ii,  p.  945,  xvii.) 

J  gives  the  heading  Officium  sci.  Gileherii  officium  per 
annum. 

Officium.  Gaudeamus.  In  J  the  title  is  Officium  sci. 
Gileberti  per  annum.  The  Mass  is  very  similar  to,  but  not 
identical  with,  that  in  Y  of  a  Confessor  not  a  Bishop.  J  does 
not  give  any  Mass  In  die  for  S.  Gilbert. 

Probably  these  variations  are  reconciled  by  the  Ordinal. 
"  Officium  ad  missam  Gaudeamus  .  ceteris  diebus  per  octauas 
et  per  annum  Os  iusti  .  .  .  sequencia  sc.  Exaltemus  uel 
Ortus  sol.

Oratio  S.  Ambrosii. 

There  is  no  heading  to  this  prayer,  which  commonly  goes 
under  the  name  of  S.  Ambrose.  The  prayer  was  originally 
written  in  a  different  hand  to  that  of  the  Collectar,  but 
contempoiaiy.  The  fiist  pait  of  it  has  been  lewiitten  by  a 
latei  hand  ;  similar  to  that  of  the  piefixed  section,  and  has 
been  ciowded  into  one  folio,  the  pieceding  leaf  being  taken 
up  with  other  matter.  The  reason  of  the  rewriting  was  to 
secure  a  different  and  shorter  recension  of  the  prayer  than 
the  original.  The  section  Suscipe  ergo  .  .  .  in  laude  ct 
gloria  tua,  wYnchoccwT?,  in  the  Cistercian  Dijon   exemplar,    is 

here  replaced  by  a  shoitei  passage,  Peto  elemeniiam 

mi'cJii  salus  et  uita.     The  Cisteician  Missal    (1584)   has  the 
two  passages  in  the  piayer.

altar  according  to  the  Cistercian  (and  Augustinian)  Use.

at  the  side.  Possibly  also  to  insert  the  form  for  the  admini- 
stration of  the  cup,  which  for  some  reason,  probably  inad- 
vertence, did  not  originally  appear.  J  agrees  in  the  matter 
and  order  of  these  prayers.

Hodie  quoque  (etc.). 

The  Collectae  de  Suffragio  must,  I  think,  be  the  usitate 
collecte  (see  above  Fer.  iiii  of  Lent  IV),  i.e.  the  Memoriae 
used  after  the  Collect  for  the  da}-.  The  similar  expression 
as  Memoriae  de  suffragiis  occurs  in  the  Ordinal,  de  missis 
matutinal  ibus  [infra  Oct.  A  see  us.).  The  name  usitate 
collecte  is  used  in  the  Cistercian  Missal,  the  collects  being 
Concede  qs.  omn.  deus  ut  intercessione,  and,  Omn.  semp.  deus 
qui  uiuorum.  Three  collects  were  commonly  said  according 
to  the  Gilbertine  use,  and  seven  on  occasion. — See  Ordinal, 
De  numero  collectarum.

The  Common,  with  its  rich  choice  of  prayers,  seems  to  have 
been  the  work  of  the  compiler  of  the  CoUectar.  There  is  no 
Common  in  the  Cistercian  Rite,  and  in  most  cases  the  com- 
piler seems  to  have  used  the  Proper  as  his  source. 

The  first  mass  Uniiis  martyris  non  Pontificis  is  found 
in  G^YH.  The  collect  of  the  third  occurs  in  the  Common  of 
C.  Brev. 

p.  206.  The    fifth    mass    of    the    same    series,    Letetur    ecclesia 

tua,  is  in  S  as  a  Missa  unius  martyris. 

The  first  mass  In  nat.  plitrimorinn  martyrum,  is  in  G*YS. 
C  Brev. 

p.  208.  The  first  mass  Unius  Confessoris  Pontificis,  is  in  G^YH,  but 

YH  have  a  different  Postc.  Beati  N.  confessoris.     Collect 
in  C.  Brev. 

p.  210.  The  collect  of  the  mass  Unius  con/,  non  pont.  is  found  in 

C.  Brev. 

The  mass  Unius  Confessoris  Abbatis  is  found  in  Y.  It  is

INDEX    OF  FORMS. 


In  the  description  of  the  forms  in  this  index,  I  have  used  the  following 
abbreviations  :  Co.  stands  for  Communio,  Coll.  for  Collecta,  Gr.  for  Graduate, 
Pref.  for  Praefatio,  Off.  for  Officium,  Offert.  for  Offertor'ium,  Or.  for  Oratio,  P.C. 
for  Post  communio,  S.  for  Secreta,  S.P.  for  Supsr  populum,  Tr.  for  Tractus. 
The  rest  are,  I  think,  obvious.
